**Introduction**  
One day, a silly hurricane came out of the sky. It blew and blew, and all the people ran away. It brought lots of rain and strong winds. The little girl watched it outside her window, but she was not scared.

**Development**  
When the hurricane was finished, she went out to play. Everything was wet, but the sun was shining again. She had a lot of fun, playing in the mud. She made mud pies and mud castles. She even sang silly songs.

**Conclusion**  
When she was done, the little girl went inside to take a nap. The silly hurricane was gone and the world was quiet again.
**Introduction**  
One day, a big automobile was stuck in the mud. It tried to move, but it could not. The driver was frustrated. He did not know what to do. He looked around for help.

**Development**  
A strong man came and said, "I can help you!" The man tried to lift the automobile, but it was too heavy. He tried again and again. The driver watched and said, "Thank you for trying." Then, a little boy came. He had a big idea. He said, "Let's use a stick to help lift the automobile." They found a big stick and put it under the automobile. The strong man and the little boy pushed the stick together. Slowly, the automobile started to lift. They pushed harder and harder.

**Conclusion**  
Finally, the automobile was out of the mud. The driver was happy and said, "Thank you for helping me!" They all smiled and waved goodbye.

**Development**

He pointed to a hidden spot behind a bush and said “Let’s pretend we are on a treasure hunt, and that hidden spot is where our treasure is!” Sarah was very excited about the idea. She said “Let’s try”, so the two friends began searching for their treasure. They crawled through the bush and scratched the ground with their fingers.

**Conclusion**  
After a while, they finally found the treasure they were looking for. It was a box full of colorful stones. Tom and Sarah were very happy and hugged each other. It was a great success, thanks to their five fingers!

**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were twins who liked to play in the garden. One day, they saw a big bird fly over their house and drop something in the grass. They ran to see what it was. It was a big egg with spots and stripes.  

**Development**  
"Wow, look at this egg!" Lily said. "I wonder what is inside." "Maybe it is a baby bird," Ben said. "Or a baby dinosaur." They decided to take the egg to their room and keep it warm. They wrapped it in a blanket and put it on their bed. They waited and waited for the egg to crack. The next morning, they heard a loud noise from their room. They ran to see what happened. The egg had broken and a strange creature had come out. It had feathers and scales, claws and wings, a beak and a tail. It looked scary and angry. It roared and snapped at them. "Help, help!" Lily and Ben screamed. "It is a monster!" They ran out of their room and called their mom and dad. Their parents came and saw the creature. They were shocked and scared too. They did not know what to do.  

**Conclusion**  
Then they heard another noise from outside. It was the big bird that had dropped the egg. It had come back to look for its baby. It saw the creature in the room and flew down to get it. It picked it up with its talons and flew away. Lily and Ben watched as the bird and the creature disappeared in the sky. They felt relieved and sorry at the same time. "Maybe we should not have taken the egg," Lily said. "Maybe we should have left it alone," Ben said. They learned a lesson that day. They learned to be careful with things they did not understand. They learned to respect nature and its mysteries. They learned to never touch a scary egg again.

**Conclusion**

He moves the net slowly and quietly. He catches the small fish in the net. "Wow, Ben, you did it!" Anna says. She claps her hands. "Can I see the fish?" Ben shows Anna the fish. It is shiny and wet. It moves in the net. "Hello, fish," Anna says. She touches the fish gently. "You are pretty." The fish does not like the net. It wants to go back to the water. It flaps its tail and makes a splash. "Uh-oh, the fish is unhappy," Ben says. He feels sorry for the fish. "Maybe we should let it go." Anna nods. She likes the fish, but she does not want to make it sad. She helps Ben to lift the net and put it back in the water. The fish swims away quickly.

**Conclusion**  
"Bye, fish," Anna and Ben say. They wave to the fish. They are happy to see the fish free. They decide to play with their net in another way. They fill it with leaves and flowers. They make a colorful net. They have fun with their new net.

He watched closely as the spider showed him how to secure the web around the nail.

**Conclusion**

**Introduction**  
Ben and Sam were brothers who liked to play outside. One day, they found a big pile of bricks near their house. They thought it would be fun to build a wall with the bricks.  

**Development**  
"Let's make a wall here," Ben said, pointing to a spot near the road. "We can pretend it is our castle." "I don't know," Sam said, feeling worried. "That is too close to the road. What if a car comes and hits our wall?" "Don't be silly," Ben said. "No car will come here. It is too quiet. Come on, help me carry the bricks." Sam did not want to make Ben angry, so he helped him carry the bricks. They stacked them up high and wide, making a wall that blocked the road. They were very proud of their wall. They sat behind it and pretended to be kings. "Look at our wall," Ben said. "It is the best wall ever. No one can break it." Suddenly, they heard a loud honk. They looked up and saw a big truck coming towards their wall. The truck driver did not see the wall until it was too late. He tried to stop, but he could not. The truck crashed into the wall, making a loud noise and a big mess. The bricks flew everywhere, hitting the truck, the road, and the boys. Ben and Sam were hurt by the bricks. They cried and screamed. Their mom heard them and ran outside. She saw the truck, the wall, and the boys. She was very scared and angry.  

**Conclusion**  
She called the ambulance and the police. They came and took the boys to the hospital. They also talked to the truck driver, who was not hurt, but very sorry. Ben and Sam had to stay in the hospital for a long time. They had many cuts and bruises. They also had to say sorry to the truck driver, the police, and their mom. They learned a hard lesson. They should have listened to Sam, who was worried. They should not have built a wall near the road. They should have played somewhere safe. The moral of the story is: You should not play with things that are dangerous. You should listen to your feelings and your friends. You should do what is right and safe.

Once upon a time, there was a farmer named Mark. Mark had a big garden where he grew all sorts of fruits, vegetables, and flowers. One day he was in the garden, weeding between the plants, when he found something very weird. It was a unique carrot! It was twice the size of an ordinary carrot and the colour was a greeny-white.  

**Development**  
Mark held the carrot in his hands and looked at it curiously. He said to himself, "This carrot is really unique. I wonder where it came from." Suddenly, from behind him, he heard a little voice say, "It came from me!" Mark was so surprised that he nearly dropped the carrot. He turned around and saw a tiny fairy sitting on a cabbage leaf. The fairy said, "I am the magical gardener. I planted that carrot in your garden. I thought it would make it more unique!" Mark smiled. He thanked the fairy for the unique carrot and asked if there was anything he could do for her in return. The fairy thought for a moment, then said, "Yes, you can give me a ride home in your car. I'm very tired and would love a lift."  

**Conclusion**  
Mark laughed and said, "Sure, hop in and I'll give you a ride." So Mark and the fairy drove off in his car, and they lived happily ever after.

"Can you help me, Sarah?" Mommy asked.

Sarah was only three years old, but she was excited to help! She looked up at Mommy and said, "Yes!" She smiled big.

Sarah was so happy that she could help. She said, "I can do it!" as she stretched her arms out to raise the diary up high in the air.

Mommy was so proud and said, "Thank you for helping me, Sarah. You are a really big help!"

**Conclusion**  
Sarah smiled, happy to have been able to help Mommy. She felt so grown-up and important, being able to help even when Mommy was so busy.

Sam saw a real, burning candle and said, "Wow! That looks really beautiful!" Tim said, "Yes, it looks like a real one, doesn't it? Now I remember that real candles need to be lit with a match!" 

**Conclusion**

**Introduction**  
Tom and Lily liked to play near the river. They liked to throw stones and watch the water splash. But they knew they had to be careful. Their mom always told them not to go too close to the edge. She said the river was fast and deep and could take them away.

**Development**  
One day, Tom and Lily found a big log near the river. They thought it would be fun to push it into the water. They took turns pushing and pulling, but the log was very heavy. They could not move it much. They got tired and bored. Lily had an idea. She said to Tom, "Let's climb on the log and pretend it's a boat. Maybe it will move then." Tom agreed. He liked to pretend. They climbed on the log and sat on it. They made boat noises and waved to the birds. But they did not notice that the log was slowly rolling. It was getting closer and closer to the edge of the river. The water was pushing it. Tom and Lily were too busy playing to see the danger. Suddenly, they heard their mom shout. She had seen them from the house. She was very alert. She ran to the river and yelled, "Tom! Lily! Get off the log! It's going to fall in the water!" Tom and Lily looked up and saw their mom. They also saw the river. It was right below them. They were scared. They tried to jump off the log, but it was too late. The log tipped over and fell into the water. Tom and Lily fell with it. They screamed. The water was cold and wet and fast. They could not swim. They could not see their mom.

**Conclusion**  
But their mom saw them. She was very brave. She jumped into the river and swam to them. She grabbed them and pulled them to the shore. She hugged them and kissed them. She was very happy. She said, "Tom! Lily! You are safe! You are okay! But you were very naughty! You should never play on the log near the river! It's very dangerous! You could have drowned!" Tom and Lily were sorry. They said, "We're sorry, mom. We didn't mean to. We were just playing. We won't do it again. We love you, mom." Their mom said, "I love you too, Tom and Lily. But you have to be careful. The river is not a toy. It's a force of nature. It can hurt you. You have to respect it." Tom and Lily learned their lesson. They never played on the log near the river again. They still liked to throw stones and watch the water splash. But they always stayed away from the edge. They always listened to their mom. They always were alert.

The dog is not happy. He wants to chase Tom and Sue. He runs to the tree and growls. He tries to reach them with his paws. "Help! Help!" Tom and Sue cry. Their mom hears them. She comes out of the house. She sees the dog and the broken box. She is angry. "Bad dog! Leave my children alone!" she yells. She picks up a stick and waves it at the dog. The dog is afraid. He runs away. He does not come back.

**Conclusion**  
Tom and Sue are safe. They hug their mom. She hugs them back. "I'm sorry, kids. That dog was harsh. He did not permit you to play with the box. He ruined your house," she says. "It's okay, mom. Maybe he can help us," Sue says. They go to the house. They look for their dad. They want to make a new house. A safe house. A house with a cell.

**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were playing with their toys in the living room. They had a lot of fun making noises and pretending to be animals. Lily had a big teddy bear and Ben had a small car. They shared their toys and laughed a lot.

**Development**  
But then, Lily saw a shiny red chair in the corner. She wanted to sit on it and see how it felt. She left her teddy bear on the floor and ran to the chair. She climbed on it and smiled. She felt very big and happy. Ben saw Lily on the chair and felt angry. He wanted to sit on the chair too. He left his car on the floor and ran to the chair. He tried to push Lily off the chair and shouted, "That's my chair! Go away!" Lily did not want to go away. She held on to the chair and shouted back, "No, it's my chair! You go away!" They both pulled and pushed and yelled. They did not see the car and the teddy bear on the floor. They tripped over them and fell off the chair. They hit their heads and knees on the hard floor. They felt a terrible pain and started to cry.

**Conclusion**  
Their mom heard their cries and came to the living room. She saw them on the floor and the chair and the toys. She was very sad and angry. She picked them up and hugged them. She said, "What are you doing? Why are you fighting? You hurt yourselves and your toys. You should be nice to each other and share. The chair is for everyone, not just for one. Do you understand?" Lily and Ben nodded and said, "We're sorry, mom. We understand. We won't fight again. We love you and each other." Mom smiled and said, "I love you too. Now, let's put some ice on your bumps and bruises and then we can read a story together. How does that sound?" Lily and Ben smiled and said, "That sounds good, mom. Thank you." It is the color of grass and leaves and frogs. Try it, you might like it." But Lily did not listen to mom. She kept complaining and crying. She threw her green lollipop on the ground. She ran to Tom and tried to grab his red lollipop. Tom did not want to share his lollipop. He said, "No, Lily. This is my lollipop. You had your own. Go away!" Tom and Lily started to fight over the red lollipop. They pulled and pushed and shouted. They did not see the big black dog that was watching them. The dog liked lollipops too. He saw the green one on the ground and ate it. Then he saw the red one in Tom and Lily's hands. He ran to them and barked loudly. He scared Tom and Lily. He snatched the red lollipop from them and ran away.

**Conclusion**  
Tom and Lily were left with no lollipops. They were sad and angry. They blamed each other for losing their lollipops. They did not want to play together anymore. They went to mom and told her what happened. Mom was not happy. She said, "You see, this is what happens when you complain and fight. You lose what you have and make others unhappy. You should have been grateful and kind. Now you have no lollipops and no fun. You have to learn your lesson." The end.

**Conclusion**

Ben felt sad and shocked. He realized he had lost his sister because he was selfish. He wished he could go to the park and say sorry. He looked at his dictionary and felt no joy. He learned a new word that day: regret.

Lucy ran to the tree and started to pick the cherries. She put them all in a basket. She looked at the cherries and decided to polish them so they would look even prettier.

**Conclusion**  
Lucy was so happy with her basket of polished cherries. She wanted to eat them, so she ran inside to get a bowl. But when she opened up the basket, she found a spoiled cherry. Lucy was disappointed. She threw the spoiled cherry away and enjoyed the other cherries.  

The end.

**Introduction**  
Lily liked to play with her toys in the bathtub. She had a duck, a frog, a boat and a ball. She made them splash and swim and talk to each other. She pretended the duck was the captain of the boat and the frog was his friend.  

**Development**  
One day, Lily saw something new in the bathtub. It was a big, shiny, round thing. It had a handle and a spout. Lily did not know what it was. She touched it and it made a noise. "Pssssst!" It sprayed water everywhere. Lily was scared and wet. "Mama, mama, come!" Lily cried. "There is a monster in the bathtub!" Mama heard Lily and ran to the bathroom. She saw Lily and the thing in the bathtub. She smiled and hugged Lily. "Don't worry, Lily. It's not a monster. It's a shower head. It's for washing your hair. It's normal. Do you want to try it?" Mama asked. Lily looked at the shower head. She saw that it was not scary. It was just a toy that made water. She nodded and smiled. "Okay, mama. I want to try it. Can the duck and the frog try it too?" Lily asked. "Of course, Lily. They can try it too. Let's have some fun with the shower head." Mama said.  

**Conclusion**  
Lily and Mama played with the shower head in the bathtub. They washed their hair and their toys. They laughed and sang. Lily liked the shower head. It was a new and fun toy. She was not scared anymore. She was happy.

**Introduction**  
Lily liked to play with her lamp. It was a brilliant lamp. It had many colors and shapes on it. When she turned it on, it made her room bright and pretty.

**Development**  
One day, Lily wanted to play a game with her lamp. She said, "Lamp, lamp, can you freeze?" She hoped the lamp would stop changing colors and shapes. She wanted to see what it looked like when it was still. But the lamp did not freeze. It kept changing colors and shapes. Lily said, "Lamp, lamp, please freeze!" She tried to touch the lamp, but it was too hot. She pulled her hand back and said, "Ouch!" Then, Lily heard a loud noise. It was her mom. She came into the room and saw Lily and the lamp. She said, "Lily, what are you doing? You can't play with the lamp. It is not a toy. It is dangerous. It can burn you or break. You have to turn it off and leave it alone."

**Conclusion**  
Lily felt sad and sorry. She said, "I'm sorry, mom. I just wanted to play a game with the lamp. I wanted to see it freeze." Mom said, "I know, Lily. But the lamp can't freeze. It is not alive. It is a thing. It can only turn on and off. If you want to play a game with something that can freeze, you can play with your stuffed animals or your blocks. They are safe and fun." Lily nodded and said, "Okay, mom. I will play with my stuffed animals and my blocks. They can freeze when I say freeze. And they can move when I say move. They are brilliant, too." Mom smiled and said, "That's right, Lily. They are brilliant. And so are you. I love you." She gave Lily a hug and a kiss and turned off the lamp. Lily said, "I love you, too, mom. Thank you for teaching me." She picked up her stuffed animals and her blocks and started to play a new game.

"What is that?" asked Cat. "I don't know," Sam replied. As the line got closer and closer, they saw that it was a huge lightning bolt, ready to strike. Sam and Cat quickly ran away from the park and found a safe place to hide. When the lightning bolt finally struck, everything shined bright and filled the sky with color. Sam and Cat were too scared to look, so they kept hiding until the bright light was gone.

**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were playing in the park. They liked to slide, swing and run. Lily had a big bow in her hair. It was pink and shiny. Ben thought it was pretty.

**Development**  
"Can I see your bow?" Ben asked Lily.  
"OK, but be careful. It is my favorite bow," Lily said. She took off her bow and gave it to Ben.  
Ben looked at the bow. He saw something shiny inside. It was a small key. He wondered what it was for.  
"Wow, what is this?" Ben said. He pulled the key out of the bow.  
Lily gasped. She saw the key too. She remembered that her grandma gave her the bow. She said it was a special bow. It had a secret.  
"Ben, give me back my bow! That is a mysterious key. It opens something very important. Grandma told me," Lily said. She reached for the bow and the key.  
But Ben was curious. He wanted to find out what the key opened. He ran away with the bow and the key.  
"Ben, stop! Come back!" Lily shouted. She ran after him.  
But Ben was faster. He ran to the other side of the park. He saw a big chest. It had a lock. He thought maybe the key would fit.  
He put the key in the lock. He turned it. The chest opened. He saw a lot of toys inside. He smiled.  
"Look, Lily, I found the treasure!" Ben said. He took out a toy car and a doll.  
Lily caught up with him. She was angry and sad. She saw her bow and the key on the ground. She saw the chest and the toys.  
"Ben, you are very bad! You took my bow and my key. You opened the chest. That is not your treasure. That is grandma's treasure. She wanted me to have it. She said it was a surprise for me. You ruined it!" Lily said. She started to cry.  
Ben felt sorry. He saw that Lily was crying. He realized he did something wrong.

**Conclusion**  
"I'm sorry, Lily. I didn't know. I was just curious. Please don't cry. Here, take your bow and your key. And you can have the toys too. I don't want them. I just want to be your friend," Ben said. He gave Lily the bow and the key. He put the toys back in the chest. He closed it.  
Lily stopped crying. She looked at Ben. She saw that he was sorry. She forgave him.  
"Thank you, Ben. I'm sorry too. I was mean to you. You can be my friend. And you can share the toys with me. Grandma would want that. She said the treasure was for fun and happiness. Come on, let's play," Lily said. She put on her bow and held the key. She smiled.  
Ben smiled too. He hugged Lily. They played with the toys. They had fun and happiness. They were friends.

Lily liked to swim in the pool. She had a thin swimsuit with pink flowers. She also had a big floaty that looked like a whale. She called it Wally. One day, Lily saw a boy in the pool. He had a thin swimsuit with blue stripes. He also had a big floaty that looked like a shark. He called it Sammy.

**Development**  
Lily wanted to play with the boy and his floaty. She swam over to him and said, "Hi, I'm Lily. This is Wally. Can we be friends?" The boy looked at her and said, "Hi, I'm Ben. This is Sammy. Sure, we can be friends. But you have to believe me. Sammy is a real shark. He can bite and swim fast. He is not a toy." Lily was scared. She did not believe him. She thought he was joking. She said, "No, he is not. He is a floaty. He is soft and has air inside. He cannot bite or swim fast. He is a toy." Ben was angry. He did not like that she did not believe him. He said, "Yes, he is. He is a real shark. Watch this." He pushed a button on his floaty. The floaty made a loud noise and moved its tail. It also opened its mouth and showed its teeth. Lily was very scared. She believed him now. She screamed and swam away. She hugged Wally and said, "Wally, help me. Sammy is a real shark. He wants to bite me." Ben laughed and said, "Ha ha, I got you. He is not a real shark. He is a floaty. He has a battery and a speaker inside. He cannot bite or swim fast. He is a toy." Lily was mad. She did not like that he tricked her. She said, "That was not funny. That was mean. You scared me. I don't want to be your friend."

**Conclusion**  
Ben felt sorry. He did not mean to scare her. He said, "I'm sorry. I was just playing. I don't want to be mean. I want to be your friend." Lily looked at him and said, "Really? You do?" Ben nodded and said, "Really. I do." Lily smiled and said, "OK. But no more tricks. OK?" Ben smiled and said, "OK. No more tricks. OK." They shook hands and said, "Friends." They played with their floaties and had fun. They learned to believe each other and be nice. They were happy.

**Development**  
The little boy was wearing a pair of pants and the butterfly flew right onto his leg. He moved around and tried to shake it off, but the butterfly stayed put. He knew if he stayed still, the butterfly might fly away. He was very persistent and sat still as he can. After a few moments, the butterfly moved a tiny bit and the little boy got excited. He thought he had won. But suddenly, the butterfly bit him right on the leg! The little boy yelped and moved away quickly. The butterfly flew away and the little boy's pants were now covered in a bite mark. 

**Conclusion**  
The little boy remembered that the butterfly was persistent too. He knew he had to be careful and maybe stop chasing them in the future.

One day, a little boy named Tom went to the park with his mom. He saw a big train with a lot of smoke coming out of it. He wanted to go closer and see the train. He asked his mom if he could go.  

**Development**  
His mom said, "Yes, you can go, but be careful. The smoke is not good for you. It is called steam. It is very hot and can hurt you. Stay away from the steam." Tom nodded and ran to the train. He saw a man in a blue hat and a red shirt. He was the driver of the train. He smiled at Tom and said, "Hello, little boy. Do you like my train?" Tom said, "Yes, I do. It is very big and fast. Can I see inside?" The driver said, "Sure, you can. But first, let me tell you something. Do you know why my train makes steam?" Tom said, "No, I don't. Why?" The driver said, "My train runs on water and coal. The water boils and makes steam. The steam pushes the wheels and makes the train move. But steam is not good for the air and the plants and the animals. It makes them sick. That is why I am trying to find a better way to run my train. A way that is healthy for everyone." Tom said, "Wow, that is very smart. How can you do that?" The driver said, "I have an idea. I want to use the sun and the wind to make electricity. Electricity can also make the train move. And it does not make steam or smoke. It is clean and healthy. But I need help to make it work. Do you want to help me?"

**Conclusion**  
Tom said, "Yes, I do. I want to help you make a healthy train. What can I do?" The driver said, "You can be my friend and learn with me. You can also tell your mom and your friends about my idea. Maybe they can help too. Together, we can make a difference." Tom said, "Okay, I will. Thank you for letting me see your train. You are very nice. I am happy to meet you." The driver said, "You are very welcome. You are very curious and kind. I am happy to meet you too." Tom and the driver became friends and worked on the healthy train project. They also had fun and learned a lot. They showed everyone that steam is not the only way to make a train move. They showed everyone that being healthy is important for everyone. They showed everyone that meeting new people can lead to great things.

One day, the fisherman was in his boat when a storm blew up. The sky was dark, and the waves were big and scary. The fisherman suffered. He tried very hard to stay safe, but he couldn't keep his boat from rocking.

**Conclusion**  
In the end, the fisherman was okay. He made it back to shore and saw some colorful fish playing in the waves. He was happy to be safe. The fisherman promised himself never to let himself get in danger again. He was thankful to be alive, and kept on searching the sea for colorful fish.
**Introduction**  
Once upon a time, there was a poor child named Tim. Tim lived with his mom in a small house. They did not have much, but they were happy.

**Development**  
One day, Tim found a tiny seed. He wanted to raise it into a big plant. Tim's mom said, "Yes, you can try." So, Tim dug a hole and put the seed in the ground. He gave it water and sun. Every day, Tim took care of the seed. Soon, it grew into a big, strong plant. 

**Conclusion**  
Tim and his mom were so happy. They had raised it together. And now, they had a beautiful plant to make their home even nicer.
**Introduction**  
Leo was eager to go on his first safari. He couldn't wait to see all the exciting animals he had been reading about in books. He asked his mom, "Please can we go on a safari now?". 

His mom smiled and said, "I'm so sorry Leo, but you're only 3 years old. You're not old enough for a safari yet. But maybe next year when you're 4, you can take a safari."

Leo was very disappointed and begged his mom to let him go on the safari. But his mom repeated, "No, Leo. You have to be 4 years old. You have to wait."

Leo continued to plead with his mother, but it was of no use. He wouldn't be going on a safari for another year.

**Development**  
Now it was a year later and Leo was so excited that he could finally go on his safari. He asked his mom, "Please, can we go on safari now?". His mom smiled and said, "Yes, you are 4 now, and you can go. Let's get ready - you can't wait to be out on the safari!"

Leo was delighted and began packing right away. He couldn't wait to go on his first safari. When they arrived at the safari grounds, Leo was so eager to explore all the animals. He ran around to each enclosure, jumping

**Conclusion**  
and laughing with joy at seeing all the different animals. Leo's dream had finally come true, and he thoroughly enjoyed every moment of his very first safari adventure.
**Introduction**  
Once upon a time there was an adorable little girl named Susie. She decided she wanted to make a pie. She went to the kitchen and got flour, sugar and other yummy things. She stirred it all together into a big bowl.  

**Development**  
Next, Susie put her special mix into a pan. Then she popped the pie into the oven. She couldn't wait for it to bake! She guessed it was nearly ready when she smelled the yummy smell from the other room.  

**Conclusion**  
Finally, Susie pulled the pie out of the oven and took a peek. It looked and smelled delicious! She cut the pie into slices and shared it with her family. They all agreed it was the most delicious pie ever! Susie was very proud of her pie. She was also very happy that everyone loved it. She felt so proud and happy that she decided to bake a pie every week!  
**Introduction**  
Ben and Lily were playing in the garden. They liked to dig and plant and water the flowers. They saw a big orange pumpkin on the vine. They wanted to make a pumpkin pie.

**Development**  
"Let's pick the pumpkin and prepare the pie," Ben said. "Okay, but we need to ask Mom first," Lily said. They ran to the house and found Mom in the kitchen. They showed her the pumpkin and asked if they could make a pie. "Sure, you can help me, but we need to be careful and not waste any part of the pumpkin," Mom said. She cut the pumpkin in half and scooped out the seeds and the pulp. She gave some seeds to Ben and Lily and told them to wash them and dry them. "These are for roasting. They are yummy and crunchy," she said. She put the pulp in a pot and told them to mash it with a fork. "This is for the filling. We need to add some sugar and spice and milk," she said. She rolled out some dough and put it in a pie dish. She poured the filling over the dough and covered it with another layer of dough. She made some holes on the top with a knife. "This is for the crust. We need to bake it in the oven for a while," she said. She put the pie in the oven and set the timer. She put the seeds on a baking sheet and sprinkled some salt on them. "These are for snacking. We need to bake them for a shorter time," she said. She put the seeds in the oven and checked the timer. 

**Conclusion**  
She smiled at Ben and Lily and hugged them. "You did a great job. We didn't waste any part of the pumpkin. We made a delicious pie and a tasty snack. I'm proud of you," she said. Ben and Lily smiled back and hugged her. "Thank you, Mom. We love you. We love pumpkin pie and pumpkin seeds," they said.

Once upon a time there were two birds, a goose and a crane. They always quarreled with each other, day and night. 

**Development**  
One day, they had quarreled so much that the crane became dizzy and the goose decided to teach her a lesson. The goose said to the crane, “I know how you can make up! We will go on a flight together.” The crane was excited and the two flew high in the sky. The crane flew so high that she felt dizzy. 

**Conclusion**  
The goose told the crane, “See, now you understand how I felt when you were quarreling all the time!” The crane was sorry and promised never to quarrel with the goose again. The two birds flew happily around the sky, holding each other’s wing and enjoying their flight.

**Development**

She started to follow the marks on the stage and soon found herself in a magical land. She kept going and going until she stumbled upon a little house. Suddenly, she heard a voice from inside. “Hello, who’s there? Are you lost?” the voice asked. The little lady looked up and said, “I’m not lost. I’m on an adventure.” The voice said, “That’s wonderful! Just remember to be careful and use the marks to guide you. Have a good adventure!” The little lady thanked the voice and continued on her way. She followed the marks until she reached the end of the stage. 

**Conclusion**

The mattress heard them and said, "I can help you. Let's all get a wash. We will be clean and everyone will love us more." So, they all went to get a wash. They came back clean and fresh.

Once upon a time, there was a little fish called Bob. Bob lived in the big blue sea and loved to explore the coral reefs around him. One day, Bob came across a very sad reef. The reef had been separated from its home and was now stuck in the open ocean.

**Development**  
Bob noticed the reef's sadness and wanted to help. He said in a gentle voice, "Don't worry, little reef. I'm here to help you get home." The reef felt joy and hope as Bob's compassionate words sunk in. Suddenly, Bob saw a large wave in the distance. He moved closer to the reef and told it to hold onto him tight. Bob swam as fast as he could against the wave. Soon, they saw beautiful coral in the distance - it was the reef's home! They reached the coral in no time.

**Conclusion**  
The reef thanked Bob for helping it back home. Bob smiled and said he was happy to help. Then, he watched as the reef happily moved back to its home amongst the coral. Bob was glad that he was able to make the reef happy.
**Introduction**  
Anna loved summer. She liked to play outside with her friends, swim in the pool, and eat ice cream. She was gifted at drawing and painting. She made beautiful pictures of flowers, animals, and people. She wanted to be an artist when she grew up.

**Development**  
But not everyone liked Anna's pictures. Her big brother, Ben, was mean to her. He said her pictures were ugly and silly. He tore them up and threw them away. He made Anna cry. He said she was a baby and should stop drawing. Anna was sad and angry. She wanted to question Ben. Why was he so mean? Why did he hate her pictures? Why did he not love her? But she was afraid of him. He was bigger and stronger than her. He yelled and hit her sometimes. She did not know what to do. One day, Anna had an idea. She found a big piece of paper and some paints. She decided to make a special picture for Ben. She worked hard and carefully. She painted a picture of Ben and her. They were smiling and hugging. They were happy and loving. She wrote "I love you, Ben" on the picture. She hoped Ben would like it and be nice to her. She put the picture on Ben's bed and waited for him to come home. She hid behind the door and watched. Ben came home and saw the picture. He looked at it for a long time. He did not tear it up or throw it away. He did not yell or hit. He smiled and cried. He picked up the picture and hugged it. He said "I love you, too, Anna". He saw Anna behind the door and ran to her. He hugged her and kissed her. He said he was sorry for being mean. He said he liked her pictures and was proud of her. He said she was gifted and should keep drawing.

**Conclusion**  
Anna was happy and surprised. She hugged Ben and kissed him. She said she forgave him and thanked him. She said she was glad he loved her. They went to Anna's room and looked at her pictures together. They laughed and talked. They were happy and loving. They were brother and sister.
**Introduction**  
Lily was a little girl who loved animals. She had many toys of different animals, but her favorite was a big gray elephant. She named him Elly and played with him every day. She liked his long trunk, his big ears, and his unique sound. She learned to call him with a loud "prrr" noise, and he would come to her.  

**Development**  
One day, Lily and her mom went to the zoo. Lily was very excited to see the real animals, especially the elephants. She brought Elly with her and held him tight. When they got to the elephant enclosure, Lily saw a group of gray giants. They looked like Elly, but much bigger and with more wrinkles. She heard them make the same "prrr" noise as Elly, and she smiled. She wanted to get closer to them, so she ran to the fence. She held Elly up and called him with a loud "prrr". She hoped the elephants would hear her and come to her. But they did not. They kept walking and eating and playing with each other. Lily felt sad and confused. Why did they not come to her? Did they not like her or Elly? She did not notice that one of the elephants had heard her and was curious. He walked slowly to the fence and looked at Lily and Elly. He saw that they were small and different, but also cute and friendly. He wanted to say hello to them, so he reached his trunk over the fence and touched Elly gently. He made a soft "prrr" noise, and smiled.  

**Conclusion**  
Lily felt something tickle her hand. She looked up and saw the elephant's trunk. She gasped and smiled. She saw his big eyes and his friendly face. She realized he wanted to be friends with her and Elly. She touched his trunk and made a soft "prrr" noise back. She felt happy and amazed. She had made a new friend. She hugged Elly and thanked him for being so unique.

**Introduction** One day, a big truck arrived at the farm. It had many boxes of food for the animals. The farmer was happy to see the truck. He opened the boxes and gave the food to the cows, the pigs, the chickens and the sheep.

**Development** But there was one box that he did not open. It had a label that said "onion". The farmer did not like onion. He thought it was a bad food. He left the box on the ground and went to his house. A little mouse saw the box. He was hungry and curious. He wanted to see what was inside. He chewed a hole in the box and found many round and white things. He thought they were cheese. He took one and bit it. But it was not cheese. It was onion. It was very strong and spicy. It made the mouse cry and sneeze. He dropped the onion and ran away. He felt very foolish. He wished he had not tried the onion.

**Conclusion** The next day, the farmer saw the hole in the box. He saw the onion on the ground. He laughed and shook his head. He said, "That was a foolish mouse. He did not know what onion was. He learned a lesson today. Onion is not for mice. Onion is for soup." He took the box and went to his kitchen. He made a big pot of onion soup. He liked it very much. He was glad he had onion.

**Conclusion**

**Introduction**  
Tim and Lily were playing outside with their ball. They liked to kick the ball and catch it. They also liked to look at the sky and see the clouds. Some clouds were white and fluffy, and some were gray and heavy.

**Development**  
"Look, Lily, that cloud is very large!" Tim said, pointing at a big gray cloud. "It looks like a big elephant!" "I see it, Tim! It is a large cloud. But it is not a nice cloud. It is a rain cloud. It will make us wet!" Lily said, frowning. They heard a loud noise. It was thunder. The large cloud was angry and made a loud sound. Then they saw a bright light. It was lightning. The large cloud was also scary and made a bright flash. "Let's go inside, Tim! The large cloud is not friendly. It is mean!" Lily said, grabbing Tim's hand. They ran inside the house. Their mom was there. She was cleaning the floor with a mop. She saw Tim and Lily. They were scared and wet. "What happened, Tim and Lily? Why are you wet and scared?" Mom asked. "There was a large cloud, Mom. It was angry and scary. It made thunder and lightning. It made rain. It made us wet!" Tim said.

**Conclusion**  
Mom hugged Tim and Lily. She gave them a towel to dry themselves. She smiled at them. "Don't worry, Tim and Lily. The large cloud is not mean. It is just cleaning the sky. Sometimes the sky needs to be cleaned, just like the floor. The rain is like the mop. It washes away the dirt and dust. The thunder and lightning are like the soap and water. They make the sky shiny and bright. After the large cloud is done cleaning, the sky will be blue and clear. Then you can see the sun and the rainbow. And you can play outside again!" Mom said. Tim and Lily looked at Mom. They felt better. They understood what Mom said. They liked the idea of seeing the sun and the rainbow. They thanked Mom for the towel and the hug. They waited for the large cloud to finish cleaning the sky. They hoped it would be soon. They wanted to play outside again.

As they were playing, Lily saw an ice cream truck. She urged Max to come with her to get some ice cream. Max barked happily and followed Lily to the truck. They got two ice creams, one for Lily and one for Max. Max's ice cream was in a bowl because he was a pet. After they finished their ice cream, Lily and Max went back to playing fetch. Max was so happy that he wagged his tail even harder. Lily noticed that Max was getting tired, so she decided to take him home.

**Conclusion**  
Max was so grateful to Lily for taking care of him on such a hot day. From that day on, Max always wagged his tail whenever he saw Lily.

Lila and Ben are friends. They like to play in the park. Lila has a hoop. She can roll it and jump in it. Ben has a ball. He can kick it and catch it.

**Development**  
One day, they see a new boy in the park. He is sitting on a bench. He looks sad. He has no toys. Lila and Ben want to make him happy. Lila goes to the boy. She smiles and says, "Hi, I am Lila. This is my hoop. Do you want to play with it?" She offers him the hoop. The boy looks at the hoop. He nods and takes it. He rolls it and jumps in it. He smiles and says, "Thank you, Lila. I am Sam. Your hoop is fun." Ben goes to the boy. He smiles and says, "Hi, I am Ben. This is my ball. Do you want to play with it?" He offers him the ball. The boy looks at the ball. He nods and takes it. He kicks it and catches it. He smiles and says, "Thank you, Ben. I am Sam. Your ball is fun."

**Conclusion**  
Lila and Ben are happy. They have a new friend. They play with the hoop and the ball. They laugh and run. They do not see the distant clouds. They do not hear the thunder. They only see each other. They are happy.

Once upon a time, there was a little girl named Lily. She loved to explore the forest behind her house. One day, she found a small, shiny rock. It felt smooth in her hand and she put it in her pocket.  

**Development**  
As she walked further, she saw a big tree with lots of apples on it. She picked one and took a bite. But something didn't feel right in her mouth. The apple was sour!  

Lily continued walking and saw a butterfly with interesting colors. She followed it until she saw a pond. She felt thirsty and went to drink some water. But when she looked down, she saw her reflection and realized her mouth was green from the sour apple. She felt embarrassed!  

**Conclusion**  
From that day on, Lily always made sure to taste her food before eating it. She learned that sometimes things that look interesting can be sour.

Once upon a time there was an old man who lived by the beach. Every day he would sit by the ocean, watching the big waves crash against the shore. One day, the old man noticed a tiny little wave that was far calmer than the others. It was a beautiful sight.  

**Development**  
The old man reached down and scooped up the small wave in his cupped hands. He then whispered something to the wave and dropped it back into the ocean. This was repeated every day with the same wave. One day, the small wave asked the old man why he was so kind to it. The old man smiled and said, "I want you to remain strong, for life always brings challenges that you must accept and endure." After hearing this, the wave thanked the old man and made a promise to remain strong when facing life's difficulties.  

**Conclusion**  
The moral of this story is that no matter how small or old you are, you can remain strong when faced with challenges.

**Conclusion**

**Development**

Laura smiled back, happy that she had reversed all the way to the fire truck. She waved goodbye and started to reverse once more.

**Conclusion**

**Conclusion**

**Conclusion**

**Introduction**  
Tim and Sam are brothers. They like to play with their toys in their room. Sometimes they make a lot of noise and their mom tells them to be quiet.

**Development**  
One day, they find a big bag of candy in the kitchen. They are very happy and sneak into their room with the candy. They open the bag and see many colors and shapes. They each take a handful and start to chew. "Wow, this is so good!" Tim says with his mouth full. "I like the red ones. They are sweet and sour." "I like the blue ones. They are minty and cool." Sam says and pops another one in his mouth. They chew and chew and chew. They do not hear their mom calling them for lunch. They do not hear their dad coming home from work. They do not hear their dog barking outside. They only hear their own chewing. Soon, the bag is empty. They have eaten all the candy. They feel sick and thirsty. They look at each other with wide eyes. They are scared. They know they have done something bad. They hear their mom knocking on their door. "Tim, Sam, are you there? What are you doing? Why are you so quiet?" They do not answer. They do not know what to say. They hide the bag under their bed. They hope their mom will not find it. They hope their mom will not be angry. They hope their mom will not notice their wide eyes and their sticky mouths.

**Conclusion**  
They sit on their bed in silence. They wish they had not eaten all the candy. They wish they had listened to their mom. They wish they had played with their toys instead.

**Development**

The creature looked up at Bob, shrugged, and said "I am scared, I cannot find my way back home". Bob thought for a moment and decided to help the creature. He was brave and offered his assistance. He said, "I can take you home if you like". The creature smiled and said, "thank you so much, that would be wonderful". Bob nodded, grabbed on to the creature with one hand and clutched a handful of roots with the other. He held on tight, and together they flew off into the night.  

**Conclusion**  
Bob returned the creature back home safely and when they said their goodbyes, the creature thanked Bob for his courage and kindness. Bob smiled and waved goodbye as the creature flew off into the night.

**Development**

Maybe they forgot it." Tommy was curious. He wanted to see what was in the basket. He ran to the bench and opened the basket. He saw a lot of food. There were apples, bananas, bread, cheese, and cookies. Tommy was hungry. He took a cookie and ate it. It was yummy. But then, he heard a voice. It was a lady. She said, "Hey, that's my basket! That's my food! What are you doing?" She came to the bench and saw Tommy with the cookie. She was angry. She said, "You are a bad boy! You are stealing my food! How dare you!" Tommy was scared. He dropped the cookie and ran to his mom. He was crying. He said, "Mom, I'm sorry. I didn't know. I was hungry. I just wanted a cookie." His mom hugged him and said, "It's okay, Tommy. I know you didn't mean to. But you should not take things that are not yours. That is wrong. You should say sorry to the lady and give her back the cookie."

**Conclusion**  
Tommy nodded. He was ashamed. He went to the lady and said, "I'm sorry, lady. I'm sorry I took your cookie. Here, you can have it back." He gave her the cookie. The lady looked at him and said, "Thank you, Tommy. I accept your apology. But next time, please ask before you take something. That is polite. And if you are hungry, you can ask me for a cookie. I have plenty. Here, you can have one." She gave him a cookie. Tommy smiled and said, "Thank you, lady. You are nice. Can I point at your basket?" The lady laughed and said, "Sure, Tommy. You can point at my basket. But only point, okay?" Tommy said, "Okay." He pointed at the basket and said, "Basket." The lady said, "Basket." They both laughed. Tommy was happy. He learned a lesson. He learned not to take things that are not his. He learned to say sorry and thank you. He learned to point and ask. He learned to be polite.

**Conclusion**

The dad saw the cut too, and he said, "Maybe the leopard had a fight with another leopard, and they split its ear. That's why it's angry and sad. It needs a friend to make it feel better." The boy nodded, and he said, "Maybe we can be its friends. We can give it some food and a toy, and talk to it nicely. Maybe then it will stop roaring and be happy." The dad smiled, and he said, "That's a good idea. Let's go and find a zookeeper, and ask if we can do that. Maybe the leopard will like us, and we can come and visit it again." 

**Conclusion**  
The boy smiled too, and he said, "Yes, let's do that. I like the leopard, even if it's scary. I want to help it." They held hands and walked away from the cage, looking for a zookeeper. The leopard watched them go, and it stopped roaring. It felt a little curious, and a little less lonely.

**Conclusion**

Max smiled and said, "Thank you, Lila and friends. You are my friends too. Can we play again?" Lila and her friends nodded and said, "Yes, we can play again. But this time, you are the seeker. Ready or not, here I come!"

He says, "But mom, my horse needs a star. He is a star horse." Mom says, "I understand, Sam, but the stamp is not yours. You can find another star for your horse. How about a sticker or a button?" Sam thinks for a moment. He remembers he has some stickers and buttons in his box. He says, "Okay, mom, I will give you back the stamp. But can I have a hug first?" Mom says, "Of course, Sam. **Conclusion**  
Sam goes to his box and finds a sticker and a button. He puts the sticker on his horse's head and the button on his saddle. He thinks his horse looks even better now. He climbs back on his horse and rocks again. He says, "Look, mom, my horse has a sticker and a button. He is a star horse." Mom says, "He sure is, Sam. He is a very lucky horse to have you." Sam smiles and says, "And I am a very lucky cowboy to have him."

They ate and ate until there was nothing left of the apple. They felt happy and full. But then they heard a loud voice. "Hey, who ate my apple?" It was Farmer Brown. He was angry. But Farmer Brown chased them with a stick. "Stop, you thieves! Give me back my apple!" Farmer Brown shouted. Bunny and Mouse were scared. They ran and ran until they reached their hole.  

**Conclusion**  
They hid inside and hoped Farmer Brown would not find them. They felt sorry for what they did. They wished they had not eaten the apple. They learned a lesson. They should not take things that were not theirs. They should ask first. They should be honest and kind. They said to each other, "We are sorry, Farmer Brown. We will not do it again."

He carefully took Take it out of the oven, and saw that it was a perfect fit. He let it cool down. Once it was finally cooled down, Jack added some decorations. He put a beautiful flower on the top of the cake. He also put some icing on the top and sides of the cake. 

**Conclusion**
Finally, Jack put the cake on a comfortable chair next to his Mom's bed. He put his heel right next to the cake so she could see it when she woke up! Jack was very proud of his special cake. He could not wait to see his Mom's reaction when she saw the cake.

**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were best friends. They liked to play together in the park, and share their toys and snacks. One day, they found a shiny crystal in the grass. It was blue and sparkly, and they both wanted it.

**Development**  
"Can I have it?" Lily asked.  
"No, it's mine!" Ben said.  
They started to tug on the crystal, and it broke in half. They both felt sad and angry.  
"I don't want to be your friend anymore!" Lily said.  
"Me neither!" Ben said.  
They ran away from each other, and sat on opposite sides of the park. They felt lonely and sorry, but they did not know how to say it.  
A nice lady saw them, and came over to talk to them. She smiled and said, "Why are you so sad, little ones?"  
Lily and Ben told her what happened. They showed her the broken crystal, and said they wanted it back.

**Conclusion**  
The lady nodded and said, "I understand. But you know, sometimes things break, and that's okay. What matters more is your friendship. You can still share the crystal, and make something beautiful with it. Do you want to try?"  
Lily and Ben looked at each other, and nodded. They felt nervous, but also curious. The lady helped them tie the crystal pieces to a string, and made a necklace. She gave it to Lily and Ben, and said, "This is a special necklace. It means you are friends forever, and you care for each other. Do you know what that is called?"  
Lily and Ben shook their heads.  
The lady smiled and said, "It's called marry. When two people love each other, and want to be together always, they marry. And they wear a ring, or a necklace, or something else, to show it. Do you want to marry?"  
Lily and Ben looked at the necklace, and then at each other. They felt happy and warm. They nodded and said, "Yes, we want to marry!"  
They hugged each other, and put on the necklace. They thanked the lady, and ran back to play. They felt like they had the most beautiful crystal in the world.

**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were playing pirates on the beach. They had a big stick for a sword, a red scarf for a flag, and a bucket for a treasure chest. They dug a hole in the sand and buried their treasure. Then they looked for a good place to hide.  

**Development**  
"Let's go behind that big rock," Ben said. "No one will find us there." They ran to the rock and crouched behind it. They pretended to watch for enemy ships and sea monsters. They made loud noises with their mouths. "Boom! Boom! Boom!" Ben shouted. "We are shooting our cannons!" "Roar! Roar! Roar!" Lily growled. "We are scaring the sharks!" They had so much fun that they did not notice a man walking on the beach. He had a long beard, a hat, and a hook for a hand. He looked like a real pirate. He saw the red scarf sticking out of the sand. He walked towards it. Lily and Ben heard his footsteps. They peeked from behind the rock. They saw his hook and his beard. They gasped. "It's a pirate!" Lily whispered. "He's going to steal our treasure!" "What do we do?" Ben asked. "Maybe we can scare him away," Lily said. "Let's make a loud noise." They picked up a big shell and threw it at the pirate. It hit him on the head. He dropped his hook and rubbed his head. He looked angry. "Who did that?" he yelled. "Show yourselves, you scurvy dogs!" Lily and Ben were scared. They did not want to face the pirate. They looked around for a way to escape. They saw a big anchor lying on the beach. It was cold and rusty. It had a rope attached to it. "Look, Ben!" Lily said. "We can use the anchor to swing away!" They grabbed the rope and tied it to the rock. Then they climbed on the anchor and pushed off. They swung over the pirate's head and landed on the other side of the beach. They ran to their treasure and dug it up. They put it in their bucket and ran away.  

**Conclusion**  
"Ha ha ha!" they laughed. "We tricked the pirate!" The pirate was confused. He saw the children swinging on the anchor and running away. He shook his fist. "Come back here, you little rascals!" he shouted. "That's my treasure!" But Lily and Ben did not hear him. They were too busy celebrating their victory. They had saved their treasure and had a great adventure. They were the best pirates ever.

After the spider was gone, the princess felt much better. But then, she heard a sad news that her favorite toy was broken and couldn't be fixed. She felt very sad and cried a lot. Her mommy hugged her and told her that it's okay to be sad and cry when something bad happens.

**Conclusion**  
The princess realized that it's okay to feel sad sometimes, but she didn't want to be sad forever. So, she decided to play with her other toys and have fun with her friends. Even though her favorite toy was dead, she knew she could still have fun and be happy.
**Introduction**  
Lila was a little girl who liked to twist and dance. She twisted her arms, her legs, her hair, and her dress. She felt happy and free when she twisted.  

**Development**  
One day, she saw a big tree in the park. It had many branches and leaves. It looked old and wise. Lila wanted to twist with the tree. She ran to it and hugged it. The tree felt Lila's hug. It had a kind spirit. It liked Lila too. It whispered to her with a soft voice. "Hello, little dancer. Do you want to twist with me?" Lila nodded. She was curious and excited. The tree said, "Hold on to my branch. I will show you how to twist with the wind." Lila did as the tree said. She held on to a branch and lifted her feet. The tree swung Lila gently. She felt the wind in her face and hair. She laughed and twisted.  

**Conclusion**  
The tree smiled and twisted too. They twisted together, happy and free.

**Development**

The bunny was amazed and started to pick up the things he saw. But suddenly he heard a loud noise and the door slammed shut. He was trapped, and the bunny felt scared. The bunny looked around and saw a strange mouse. The bunny said to the mouse, "Can you help me? I want to get out!" The mouse thought and then said, "Yes! Follow me." So the bunny followed the mouse and they came to a window. The mouse opened it and the bunny was so happy when he felt the cool air in his fur. He hopped through the window and out into the wild!

**Conclusion**  
The bunny was thankful to the mouse. He said, "Thank you for being so helpful and getting me out!" The mouse smiled and said, "You are welcome, tiny bunny. Just remember to close the big door next time you want to explore!" The bunny promised that he would and smiled as he hopped away into the wild.

**Development**

The goat was very proud of himself for helping the farmer and loved seeing all the animals happily playing in the field. One day, a big storm came and the winds were very strong. **Conclusion**

**Development**

After a few days, the butterfly came back and they caught it.

**Conclusion**  
The moral of the story is that if you want something, you have to work for it. And sometimes, you have to think of creative solutions to achieve your goals.

**Development**

Tom runs to her and tries to help her. He takes her hand and sees many little spikes in it. He pulls them out one by one. Lila still cries. "I told you not to touch it. Why did you not listen?" Tom says. Lila is sorry. She says she was curious. She says she is sorry. Tom is grumpy. He says she should be more careful. He says he is mad at her. Lila feels bad. She says she loves him. She says she wants to hug him. Tom is still grumpy. He says he does not want to hug her. He says he wants to lie down. Lila follows him. She lies down next to him. She puts her arm around him. She says she is sorry again.

**Conclusion**  
Tom feels her arm. He feels her warm. He feels her soft. He feels her love. Tom is not grumpy anymore. He says he loves her too. He says he is sorry too. Lila and Tom hug each other. They smile. They are happy. They forget about the cactus.

**Development**

Ella ran inside and asked her mum, "What is that bright circle in the sky?" Her mum looked up and smiled. She explained, "That is a rainbow, Ella. It is formed when the sun shines through the raindrops, but it only appears for a short time." However, Ella still didn't understand. She still wanted to know how the rainbow was made. Her mum tried again to explain, but Ella still wasn't convinced. Suddenly, Ella had an idea. She ran outside and waved her arms at the sky, trying to make the rainbow stay for longer. But, of course, it didn't work and soon the rainbow disappeared.  

**Conclusion**  
Ella went back inside and looked at her mum, who was laughing. She explained, "Ella, it's not that easy to make the rainbow stay. It will always come and go quickly." Ella had to accept that she couldn't make the rainbow stay, but she was happy to have seen it and to have learned how it was made too.

He thought he could make his structure bigger and better than Ben's. But he did not notice that he was making Ben's structure weaker and unstable. When Ben came back, he saw what Sam had done. He was angry and sad. He shouted at Sam, "Why did you take my blocks? You ruined my structure! It was so nice and now it is disgusting!"

**Conclusion**

I was jealous of your structure and I wanted to have it. But I should have been happy for you and asked you to teach me how to build it. Can you forgive me?" Ben looked at Sam and saw that he was sincere. He said, "I forgive you, Sam. But you have to promise me that you will not do that again. And you have to help me fix my structure and make it nice again." Sam nodded and hugged Ben. He said, "I promise, Ben. And I will help you. And maybe we can build a structure together, as brothers." She goes fast and feels the wind in her hair. She laughs and screams. She likes the slide. Ben watches Lily. He sees her go down the twist. He thinks it looks weird. He wonders what it feels like. Ben takes a deep breath. He sits on the slide and holds the sides. He looks at Lily. She nods and smiles. Ben pushes himself and starts to slide. He goes fast and feels the wind in his face. He gasps and closes his eyes. He does not like the slide. He goes down the twist. He feels dizzy and weird. He wants to stop. He opens his eyes and sees the ground. He is almost there. He is glad. Ben reaches the end of the slide and falls off. He lies on the grass and breathes hard. He does not laugh or scream. He does not like the slide. Lily runs to Ben. She sees him on the grass. She thinks he is hurt. She is worried. "Ben, Ben, are you okay?" Lily asks. She kneels next to him and touches his arm. "Did you like the slide?" Ben looks at Lily. He shakes his head. He does not like the slide. He does not want to try it again.

**Conclusion**  
"No, I did not like the slide," Ben says. He sits up and rubs his head. "It was too high and too long and too twisty. It made me feel weird. I do not want to slide anymore." Lily frowns. She likes the slide. She wants to slide again. She does not understand why Ben does not like it. "But it was fun!" Lily says. She stands up and points to the slide. "It was fast and cool and exciting. It made me feel happy. I want to slide more." Ben shakes his head again. He does not want to slide more. He wants to do something else. "Can we play something else?" Ben asks. He stands up and looks around. He sees a swing, a sandbox, and a seesaw. He likes those things. "Can we swing or dig or balance?" Lily thinks. She likes those things too. But she likes the slide more. She wants Ben to like it too. She has an idea. "Okay, we can play something else," Lily says. She smiles and takes Ben's hand. "But first, can we slide one more time? Please?" Ben hesitates. He does not want to slide one more time. He is scared and weird. But he likes Lily. She is his friend. He does not want to make her sad. "Okay, okay, we can slide one more time," Ben says. He sighs and follows Lily. "But only one more time. And then we play something else. Okay?" Lily nods and squeezes Ben's hand. She is happy. She thinks Ben will like the slide this time. She thinks it will be fun. They climb the stairs and sit on the slide. They hold the sides and look at each other. They are ready. They push themselves and start to slide. They go fast and feel the wind. They laugh and scream. They like the slide.

**Introduction**  
Lily and Tom were friends who liked to play in the park. One day, they saw a large peach tree with many ripe peaches on it. They wanted to pick some peaches and eat them.

**Development**  
"Look, Tom, a large peach tree!" Lily said. "Let's come and get some peaches!" "OK, Lily, but we have to be careful. The tree belongs to Mr. Lee, and he does not like children to take his peaches. He might get angry and yell at us." Tom said. They ran to the tree and climbed up. They picked some peaches and put them in their pockets. They were about to climb down when they heard a loud voice. "Hey, you two! What are you doing on my peach tree? Get down from there right now!" It was Mr. Lee, who had seen them from his window. Lily and Tom were scared and dropped their peaches. They climbed down quickly and ran away. Mr. Lee followed them and caught them. "I told you not to touch my peaches! They are not for you! They are for me and my family! You are very naughty and greedy!" Mr. Lee said. He took their peaches and threw them away. Lily and Tom felt sorry and ashamed. They realized they had done something wrong. They apologized to Mr. Lee and promised not to do it again. "I'm sorry, Mr. Lee. We should not have taken your peaches. We were hungry and curious. We will not do it again." Lily said. "Me too, Mr. Lee. We were wrong. We should have asked you first. We will not come to your tree again." Tom said.

**Conclusion**  
Mr. Lee saw that they were sincere and felt sorry for them. He decided to forgive them and teach them a lesson. "It's OK, Lily and Tom. I accept your apology. But you have to learn to respect other people's property and ask for permission before you take something. Here, take these peaches. They are for you. But next time, if you want some peaches, come and ask me nicely. I will be happy to share them with you." Mr. Lee said. He gave them each a peach and smiled. Lily and Tom thanked him and smiled back. They learned their lesson and became friends with Mr. Lee. They also shared their peaches with other children in the park. They were happy and grateful. The moral of the story is: Do not take what is not yours without asking. Respect other people's property and feelings. Be honest and apologize when you make a mistake. Be generous and share what you have with others.

**Development**  
But on Wednesday morning, it rained a lot. Mom said they had to delay their visit. She said the road was wet and slippery, and it was not safe to drive. Anna and Ben were sad and angry. They wanted to see Grandma. "Can we call Grandma and say hello?" Anna asked. "Sure, we can do that," Mom said. She picked up the phone and dialed Grandma's number. "Hello, Grandma, it's Anna and Ben," Anna said. "Hello, my darlings, how are you?" Grandma said. "We are fine, but we can't come today. It's raining and Mom says we have to delay," Ben said. "Oh, I'm sorry to hear that. I was looking forward to seeing you. But I understand. It's better to be safe than sorry," Grandma said. "Can we come tomorrow, Grandma?" Anna asked. "Of course, you can. I'll be waiting for you with cookies and hugs. And maybe the sun will come out tomorrow," Grandma said. "OK, Grandma, we love you. Bye-bye," Anna and Ben said. "Bye-bye, I love you too. See you tomorrow," Grandma said.

**Conclusion**  
Mom hung up the phone and smiled at Anna and Ben. "Grandma is very nice, isn't she?" Mom said. "Yes, she is. She is the best Grandma in the world," Anna and Ben said. They gave Mom a hug and went to play with their toys. They hoped the rain would stop soon. They couldn't wait to visit Grandma. She was elderly, but she was fun.
**Introduction**  
Sara and Ben were twins who liked to play with their toys. One day, they found a big box in the attic. Inside the box, there were many old things. Sara saw a shiny wand and a hat with stars. Ben saw a book with pictures and a cape with a hood.  

**Development**  
"Let's play magic!" Sara said. She put on the hat and took the wand. Ben put on the cape and opened the book. They pretended to be wizards and witches. They waved the wand and said funny words. They looked at the book and pointed at the pictures. But something strange happened. The wand and the book were really magic! They made things come true. Sara turned the sofa into a frog. Ben made the lamp fly. They laughed and clapped. They did not know they were in trouble. Mom and Dad came home and saw the mess. They were angry and scared. They tried to fix the sofa and the lamp, but they could not. They were helpless. They did not know what to do.  

**Conclusion**  
Sara and Ben felt sorry. They did not mean to make Mom and Dad sad. They wanted to help. They looked at the wand and the book. They remembered what they said and did. They tried to undo the magic. They waved the wand and said sorry. They pointed at the book and said stop. The magic worked. The sofa turned back into a sofa. The lamp fell down. The frog hopped away. The wand and the book stopped glowing. Mom and Dad hugged Sara and Ben. They were happy and relieved. They thanked them for fixing the magic. Sara and Ben smiled. They learned a lesson. They decided to join Mom and Dad and help them clean the attic. They put the wand, the book, the hat and the cape back in the box. They did not play with them again. They played with their own toys. They had fun without magic.

**Introduction**  
Ben and Lily were playing in the garden. They liked to dig in the dirt and look for bugs. Sometimes they found worms, snails, or ants. They put them in a jar and watched them.  

**Development**  
"Look, Lily, I found a beetle!" Ben said. He held up a shiny black bug with long legs and horns. "It is rough and hard. Do you want to touch it?" Lily shook her head. She did not like beetles. They were scary and ugly. She liked butterflies and ladybugs. They were pretty and soft. "No, thank you, Ben. I don't want to touch it. You can keep it." Ben smiled. He liked beetles. They were cool and strong. He put the beetle in the jar with the other bugs. He closed the lid and shook the jar gently. He wanted to see the beetle move. But the beetle did not move. It was still and quiet. Ben frowned. He opened the lid and poked the beetle with his finger. It did not move. It was not a beetle. It was a toy. "Surprise!" Lily said. She giggled and clapped her hands. She had tricked Ben. She had put the toy beetle in the dirt when he was not looking. She thought it was funny. Ben was not happy. He felt silly and angry. He did not like to be tricked. He threw the toy beetle on the ground and stomped on it. He broke it into pieces. He said, "That was not nice, Lily. That was mean. You are a bad sister."  

**Conclusion**  
Lily stopped giggling. She felt sorry and sad. She did not mean to make Ben angry. She wanted to make him laugh. She said, "I'm sorry, Ben. I was just joking. I didn't know you would be mad. Please don't be mad. You are a good brother." Ben looked at Lily. He saw her tears and heard her voice. He felt sorry and sad too. He did not mean to hurt her feelings. He wanted to make her happy. He said, "I'm sorry, Lily. I was just mad. I didn't know you were joking. Please don't cry. You are a good sister." Ben and Lily hugged each other. They said they were sorry and they loved each other. They picked up the pieces of the toy beetle and threw them in the trash. They decided to play with the real bugs instead. They were more fun and less trouble.

He began to climb and when he reached the top of the huge mountain, he looked around. Suddenly, he noticed a big rock and next to it was something shiny and silver. He walked closer and revealed that it was an old chest! He opened the chest and was shocked to discover a big, beautiful lung! It glowed and shimmered in the sun. The bear couldn't believe it! He had found the lung of a dragon!

**Conclusion**

"Can we go outside and see it?" Tom asked. He wanted to get closer to the fire.  

"No, no, no!" Mom said. She came to the window and pulled them away. "The fire is very hot and dangerous. You have to stay inside and be safe."  

"But why?" Tom questioned. He did not understand why they could not go outside.  

"Because the fire can hurt you and burn you. And the smoke can make you cough and sick. And the firemen have to work hard to stop the fire. You have to listen to Mom and stay inside." Mom explained.  

Lily and Tom were sad and curious. They wanted to see the fire, but they also wanted to be safe and healthy.  

**Conclusion**

Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tim. Tim was sad and miserable because it was dark and he could not play outside. He sat by the window and looked at the dark sky.

**Development**  
Tim's mom saw him and said, "Why are you sad, Tim?" Tim said, "It's dark and I can't play outside." His mom smiled and said, "We can celebrate and have fun inside the house!" Tim's mom turned on the lights and they danced and laughed together. They played games and had a lot of fun. The dark was not so bad anymore.

**Conclusion**  
In the end, Tim was no longer miserable. He learned that even when it is dark, he can still have fun and celebrate with his mom. And they lived happily ever after.
**Introduction**

**Development**  
His mommy said that they needed to take the car to a mechanic to fix it. The little boy didn't know what a mechanic was, but he believed that they could fix his car. They took the car to a mechanic who was very gifted at fixing cars. The little boy watched as the mechanic worked on his car. The mechanic said that he had fixed the car and the little boy was very happy. But when they got home, the car still didn't work. The little boy was very sad again. 

**Conclusion**  
His mommy said that they couldn't fix it anymore and they had to throw it away. The little boy cried and cried because he loved his car so much.

Jane and her friend kept on walking and went to buy some ice cream. As they ate, Jane showed the tape to her friend. But it was so disgusting, her friend quickly threw it away!

**Conclusion**  
Jane was so sad. She had done something naughty and her friend was mad at her. She had learned a lesson not to take things that look disgusting!

**Introduction**  
Once upon a time, there was a rich mule called Jack. He lived in a beautiful garden and loved to touch the lush, green grass. One day, a young boy came to the garden to visit. The boy was only three years old and curious. He looked at Jack and said "Hello mule! Do you enjoy touching the grass?" 

**Development**  
Jack replied, "Yes I do! Grass feels so nice and cool between my toes." 

The boy then asked, "Can I touch you to see how soft you are?" 

Jack said, "Yes! I would love that! You will find that I am very soft and warm to touch." 

The curious little boy happily patted Jack all over, feeling the soft fur and warm skin. When the boy was done, Jack thanked him for the affection. 

The boy smiled and said, "Your fur is so soft! You must be a very rich mule!" 

**Conclusion**  
Jack laughed and said, "Yes, I am indeed a very rich mule! But the best part is that now I have a new friend!" 

The boy and the mule spent the rest of the afternoon talking and enjoying each others company. They both had a great day, touching one another and learning about each other.
**Introduction**  
Lily was a cat who liked to have a collar. She had a pink collar with a bell that made a nice sound when she moved. She felt pretty and happy with her collar.

**Development**  
One day, she met a new cat in the park. His name was Tom and he was big and black. He did not have a collar. He said to Lily, "Why do you have that thing on your neck? It looks silly and noisy. You should take it off and be free like me." Lily did not like what Tom said. She said, "No, I like my collar. It is not silly, it is delicate and pretty. It shows that I have a home and a family who love me. And the bell helps me find them when I get lost." Tom did not understand. He said, "But you are a cat. You do not need a home or a family. You can go wherever you want and do whatever you want. You do not need a bell, you have your nose and ears. Come with me and I will show you how to have fun." Lily did not want to go with Tom. She said, "No, thank you. I have enough fun with my home and my family. They play with me and feed me and cuddle me. They are my friends. You are not my friend. You are rude and mean. Go away and leave me alone." Tom was angry and tried to grab Lily's collar. But Lily was fast and smart. She scratched Tom's face and ran away.

**Conclusion**  
She found her family and told them what happened. They hugged her and praised her. They said, "You are a brave and good cat. We are proud of you. We love you and your collar. It is part of you and it makes you special." Lily was happy and purr.

She put the cheese in different bags, and wrote the names of the cheese on the bags. She had yellow cheese, white cheese, soft cheese, hard cheese, and many more.

**Development**  
One day, Anna wanted to make a cheese sandwich for lunch. She opened the fridge and looked for her cheese box. But she could not find it. She looked everywhere, but the cheese box was gone. She felt very sad and angry. Who took her cheese box? She heard a noise from the living room. She ran there and saw her little brother, Ben, sitting on the floor. He had her cheese box, and he was eating her cheese. He had opened all the bags, and mixed all the cheese together. He had cheese on his hands, on his face, and on the floor. "Ben, what are you doing?" Anna shouted. "That is my cheese! You took my cheese box! You made a big mess! Give it back to me!" Ben looked at Anna and smiled. He liked cheese too, but he did not care about the names or the bags. He just wanted to taste all the cheese. He did not understand why Anna was so mad.

**Conclusion**  
"Cheese is good," he said. "Cheese is yummy. Come, Anna, have some cheese with me. Cheese is fun!"

**Conclusion**

Before they could continue their discussion, they noticed a hungry bird near them. Lucy immediately became compassionate. She took the orange and gently placed it on the ground near the bird. **Conclusion**
