**Introduction**  
Sara and her mom were going to the airport. They had big bags with them. Sara was excited to see the planes and the people. She wanted to open her bag and get her teddy bear.  

**Development**  
"Mom, can I open my bag?" Sara asked. "Not now, sweetie. We have to check in our bags first. Then you can open your bag and play with your teddy bear," mom said. They walked to a big desk. A lady took their bags and gave them tickets. Sara saw a sticker on her ticket. It had a picture of a plane and a number. "What is this, mom?" Sara pointed to the sticker. "That is your seat number, Sara. It tells us where to sit on the plane. We have to find our gate number now. It tells us where to board the plane," mom explained. They followed the signs and found their gate number. They waited for a while. Sara saw many planes outside the window. She heard them make loud noises. She opened her bag and hugged her teddy bear. "Are you ready to fly, Sara?" mom asked. "Yes, mom. I am ready. But I am a little scared. Will it be safe?" Sara said. "Don't worry, Sara. Flying is safe and fun. You will see the clouds and the sky. And we will be with each other. You can hold my hand and your teddy bear. It will be comfortable," mom said. They heard an announcement. It was time to board the plane. They showed their tickets to a man. He smiled and said hello.  

**Conclusion**  
They walked to the plane and found their seats. Sara put her bag under the seat. She buckled her seat belt. She looked out the window. She saw the plane move and take off. She felt the plane go up and up. She saw the ground get smaller and smaller. She saw the clouds and the sun. She smiled and said to her mom: "Mom, I like flying. It is fun. Thank you for taking me to the airport."

**Development**

When she came back to get them, they were all crumpled and wet. She was sad and didn't know what to do. Her mom came to her and told her that it's okay to make mistakes and that she can always fold the paper and try again. Lily tried to fold the paper, but it was too wet and it ripped. She was even more sad now.

**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were playing with their toy cars on the floor. Lily had a red car and Ben had a blue car. They made noises as they zoomed their cars around the room. Lily liked her car, but she also liked Ben's car. She wanted to play with both cars.

**Development**  
"Can I have your car, Ben?" Lily asked.  
"No, it's mine. You have your own car," Ben said.  
"But I like your car too. It's so shiny and fast," Lily said.  
"Too bad. You can't have it," Ben said.  
Lily felt jealous. She wanted to make Ben share his car with her. She had an idea. She saw an arrow on the wall. It was part of a picture that showed a road with signs. The arrow pointed to the right.  
"Look, Ben, there's an arrow on the wall. It tells us where to go," Lily said.  
"Where?" Ben asked.  
"Over there. Follow me," Lily said.  
She picked up her car and ran to the wall. She pretended to follow the arrow with her car. Ben followed her with his car. He was curious.  
"What's over there?" Ben asked.  
"Something fun. You'll see," Lily said.  
She smiled. She had a plan. She wanted to turn the arrow so that it pointed to the left. Then she would tell Ben to follow the arrow again. But when he did, she would grab his car and run away. She thought it was a clever trick.  
She reached the wall and put her car down. She looked around. She saw a chair near the wall. She climbed on the chair and reached for the arrow. She tried to turn it, but it was stuck. She pulled harder, but it didn't move. She pulled even harder, but it still didn't move. She pulled so hard that she lost her balance and fell off the chair. She landed on the floor with a thud.  
"Ow!" Lily cried.  
Ben heard her cry and ran to her. He saw her on the floor, holding her head. He felt sorry for her.  
"Are you okay, Lily?" Ben asked.  
He put his car down and helped her up. He hugged her and said, "I'm sorry you got hurt. Here, you can play with my car. I don't mind."

**Conclusion**  
Lily felt ashamed. She realized that she had been mean and greedy. She didn't want to trick Ben anymore. She wanted to be his friend.  
"Thank you, Ben. You're very nice. I'm sorry I wanted your car. I like your car, but I like you more," Lily said.  
She hugged him back and said, "Can we play together?"  
"Sure, we can play together. We can share our cars. That's more fun," Ben said.  
They smiled and picked up their cars. They zoomed their cars around the room again. They followed the arrow on the wall, but they didn't care where it pointed. They only cared about each other. They were happy.
**Introduction**  
Once upon a time, there was a seal who lived in the sea. He was very soft and furry. He wanted to find someone to support him in his life but he had no one.  

**Development**  
One day, he was swimming around when he saw a big boat in the distance. He swam closer to check it out. The people on the boat were very friendly and waved to the seal. The seal was so happy to have found someone to be friends with. He swam closer and the people gave him some food. From that day on, the seal was sure he had found the support he had been looking for. He visited the people on the boat every day after that and they were very kind to him.  

**Conclusion**  
They became his best friends and they always had each other's backs. The seal was so happy to finally have found the love and support he needed.
**Introduction**  
Lily and Ben were friends who liked to play in the woods. They found a creek with clear water and many rocks. They decided to make a show for the animals who lived there.

"I will be a singer and you will be a dancer," Lily said to Ben. "We will perform for the fish and the frogs and the birds."

"OK, but we need some music," Ben said. He looked around and saw a stick and a can. He picked them up and banged them together. "How is this?"

Lily clapped her hands. "That is good. Now follow me." She jumped on a rock and began to sing a song she made up. Ben followed her and danced with the stick and the can.

**Development**  
The fish and the frogs and the birds watched them with curiosity. They liked the show and made noises to cheer them on. Lily and Ben felt happy and proud.

They did not know that a man was watching them from behind a tree. He was a poor man who had no home and no food. He was hungry and cold. He saw the stick and the can and thought they were valuable. He wanted to take them from the children.

He waited until they finished their show and ran to the creek. He grabbed the stick and the can and ran away. Lily and Ben saw him and shouted.

"Hey, that is ours! Give it back!" they cried.

But the man did not listen. He ran faster and disappeared into the woods. Lily and Ben felt sad and angry. They had lost their music.

The fish and the frogs and the birds felt sorry for them. They decided to help them. They made noises to call other animals. Soon, a squirrel, a rabbit, a deer and a bird came to the creek. They brought nuts, berries, grass and feathers. They gave them to Lily and Ben as gifts.

**Conclusion**  
"Thank you, thank you!" Lily and Ben said. They smiled and hugged the animals. They felt happy again.

They ate the nuts and berries and made a crown with the grass and feathers. They put it on Lily's head. They used the stick and the can as a scepter and a shield. They pretended to be a queen and a knight.

They performed a new show for the animals. They sang and danced and acted. The animals watched them with joy and clapped their paws and wings.

They all had fun and became friends. They played until the sun went down. Then they said goodbye and went to their homes. Lily and Ben promised to come back and visit the creek and the animals. They walked hand in hand, feeling happy and grateful.

**Conclusion**

"We are playing," says Lily. 

"We are animals," says Ben. 

The judge shakes his head. He does not like their game. He thinks they are silly and loud. 

"You are not animals," he says. "You are children. You should act like children. You should be quiet and polite and respectful. You should not run and jump and make noise. You should sit and read and learn." 

Lily and Ben do not like the judge. They think he is mean and boring. They do not want to sit and read and learn. They want to run and jump and make noise. They want to play. 

They look at each other. They have an idea. They smile. 

They run away from the judge. They run to the other side of the park. They run to the pond. They see some ducks. They pretend to be ducks. 

"Quack, quack, quack!" they say. They splash and swim and nibble on bread. They have fun. 

**Conclusion**

Lily and Ben see him. They laugh. They do not care what he says. They are happy. They are animals. They are children. They are free.

She quickly looked around for something she could do to make the flower happy again and eventually, she found the perfect thing - a brightly coloured, shiny pebble.

**Conclusion**  
Lucy placed the pebble in the flowerpot next to the flower. The flower perked up, happy to have something new to look at. Lucy was so pleased she knew just what to do to make the flower happy again. She smiled with joy and went inside to play, happy that everything was alright again.

**Development**  
Ben wanted to catch a crab. He ran after one and tried to grab it. But the crab was too quick and pinched his finger. Ben cried out and let go of the crab. He was angry and kicked the rock. The rock hurt his foot and he cried more.  

**Conclusion**  
Lily saw what happened and felt sorry for Ben. She ran to him and gave him a hug. She said, "Don't kick the rock, Ben. It's not nice. The crab was scared of you. That's why it pinched you. Let's find a nice crab to look at." She took Ben by the hand and led him to a wide pool of water. There they saw a big crab with a blue shell. It was hiding under a seaweed. Lily said, "Look, Ben. This crab is beautiful. It's not mean. It just wants to be safe. Let's watch it and not touch it." Ben and Lily sat down and watched the crab. They saw it come out of the seaweed and walk around the pool. They saw it find a small fish and eat it. They saw it wave its claws and eyes. They smiled and clapped. They forgot about the pain and had fun. Mom and Dad came and joined them. They all enjoyed the beach and the crabs.

**Conclusion**  
The dark cow never blinked again. The mean wolf had a snack, and the meadow was sad.

Faster and faster he went, he loved the wind through his hair. Suddenly he hit a bump and his bike flipped into the air. He fell off and rolled into a bed of soft grass. 

He laid there for a few minutes to check he was ok. He stood up and looked around. He could see a path winding through a forest. He followed it, turning left and right, until he arrived at the edge of the lake. 

The view of the lake was great. A family of ducks were paddling around, searching for food. Tim smiled, picked up a few stones and began throwing them into the water. 

The ducks all dived and began to swim around. Tim laughed and shouted in delight. 

**Conclusion**  
Yes... adventure can be pretty great.

**Introduction**  
Mia and Leo were twins who liked to play in the garden. They had a small house with a big yard, where they grew many flowers and vegetables. Mia loved the roses and the carrots, but Leo liked the sunflowers and the cauliflower. He said cauliflower was like a white cloud that you could eat.

**Development**  
One day, they heard a loud noise from the garden. They ran outside and saw a big truck parked near the fence. A man with a hat and a shovel was digging a hole in the ground. He was going to build a new road next to their house. "Hey, what are you doing?" Leo shouted. "You are ruining our garden!" The man looked at him and smiled. "Sorry, kid, but this is my job. I have to make way for the road. Don't worry, I'll be done soon." "But you can't do that!" Mia cried. "You are hurting our flowers and vegetables. They are our friends. We take care of them and they make us happy." The man shrugged. "Sorry, kid, but flowers and vegetables are not friends. They are just plants. They don't feel anything. They don't matter." Leo and Mia felt very sad and angry. They loved their garden and they knew their plants were alive and special. They wanted to stop the man, but they were too small and scared. They watched as the man dug deeper and deeper, until he reached the cauliflower patch. He lifted his shovel and was about to smash the white clouds. "NO!" Leo screamed. He ran towards the man and grabbed his leg. He tried to pull him away from the cauliflower. "Please, please, don't hurt them. They are my favorite. They are so humble and soft and yummy. Please, let them be."

**Conclusion**  
The man was surprised by Leo's courage and passion. He looked down at the boy who was trembling and crying. He felt a pang of guilt and pity. He realized he had been wrong. He had been cruel and careless. He had forgotten that plants were living things too. He had forgotten that children had feelings and dreams. He put down his shovel and knelt down. He gently patted Leo's head and said, "I'm sorry, kid. I'm sorry for what I did. You are right. Your cauliflower is humble and soft and yummy. And you are brave and kind and smart. You taught me a lesson today. You taught me to respect and appreciate nature. You taught me to be a better person." He hugged Leo and Mia and said, "How about we make a deal? I won't touch your cauliflower or any other plant in your garden. And you can help me plant some new flowers and vegetables along the road. That way, we can make the world more beautiful and happy. What do you say?" Leo and Mia smiled and nodded. They were happy that the man had changed his mind and his heart. They were happy that their garden was safe and their cauliflower was spared. They were happy that they had made a new friend. They agreed to help the man and share their plants with him. They thanked him for his kindness and forgiveness. They said, "You are welcome. And thank you. You are humble and soft and yummy too."

"These are herbs," she said. "Mommy uses them to cook." She picked a leaf and gave it to Ben. "Maybe this is the key," she said. Ben took the leaf and put it in the lock. He turned it, but nothing happened. He tried another leaf, and another, but the door stayed locked. "This is not working," he said. He threw the leaves on the ground. "Maybe we need to ask someone," Lily said. She saw a man walking by. He had a hat and a big bag. "Excuse me, sir," she said. "Do you know how to unlock the door?" The man stopped and looked at them. He smiled. "Hello, children," he said. "I'm the gardener. I take care of the plants and flowers. This door leads to my shed, where I keep my tools and seeds." "Can we see inside?" Ben asked. "Sure, why not," the man said. He took out a shiny key from his pocket and unlocked the door. He opened it and invited them in.

**Conclusion**  
Ben and Lily followed him. They saw many things in the shed: shovels, rakes, hoses, pots, bags, boxes, and more. They also saw more herbs hanging from the ceiling. "Wow, this is amazing," Ben said. "Thank you for showing us," Lily said. "You're welcome," the man said. "But remember, don't touch anything without asking. Some of these things are sharp or heavy or dirty. And don't try to unlock the door with herbs. They are for eating, not for opening." Ben and Lily nodded. They learned something new today. They thanked the man again and went back to the garden. They decided to play with the flowers and bugs instead. They had fun.
**Introduction**  
Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Timmy. Timmy loved to play in the jungle with his friends. One day, he saw a big crocodile in the river. The crocodile had a brilliant green color and sharp teeth.

**Development**  
Timmy shouted, "Wow, look at that brilliant crocodile!" His friend, Sarah, said, "Be careful! Crocodiles are dangerous. They can bite you and hurt you." Timmy replied, "I know, but I want to see it up close." As Timmy got closer to the river, he noticed that the crocodile was not moving. It was just lying there, not even blinking. Timmy said, "What's wrong with the crocodile? Why isn't it moving?" Sarah said, "I think it's dead. It must have rotted in the sun for too long." Timmy felt sad for the crocodile. 

**Conclusion**  
He said, "We should bury it and give it a proper goodbye." So, Timmy and Sarah dug a hole and buried the crocodile. They said a little prayer for it and promised to always respect the animals in the jungle.

**Conclusion**  
May was very sad, and she learned that it's important to be careful when you peek at the sea.

He rubbed his eyes and looked around. The noise came from a huge monster. It was the size of ten boats. The pirate was so brave that he grabbed his sword and got ready to fight. He was getting ready to battle the monster when he woke up!

**Conclusion**  
The pirate had been dreaming. He realised it was all a dream. But before he could go back to sleep, he realised something else. The monster was real! It was a tall pirate ship on the horizon! He was about to have a real adventure!

But Bob said no. He wanted to keep the screen to himself.  

Anna felt sad, and said, "I'm sorry I offered to share something with you."  
Bob just shrugged and said, "It's okay."  

Days went by and Bob wanted to play a game on the screen. He wished he had taken Anna's offer but it was too late.  

**Conclusion**  
He sadly said, "I regret not taking Anna's offer. I should have shared it with her."  
Anna smiled and said, "It's okay. I always knew you would realize that sharing is better. We can still play together."  
Bob smiled and they had lots of fun together.

**Introduction**  
Tom and Sam were brothers who liked to play with blocks. They had many blocks of different colors and shapes. They liked to build towers and bridges and castles with their blocks. Sometimes they shared their blocks, but sometimes they fought over them.

**Development**  
One day, they decided to see who could build the most impressive tower with their blocks. They split their blocks into two piles, one for each of them. They started to build their towers on opposite sides of the room. They used a measure to see how tall their towers were. They wanted to make their towers taller and taller. "Look at my tower, it is so impressive!" Tom said. "It is much taller than yours!" "No, it is not!" Sam said. "My tower is more impressive! It has more colors and shapes!" They argued and argued. They did not listen to each other. They did not care about each other's feelings. They only cared about their own towers. They kept adding more blocks to their towers, until they ran out of blocks. They looked at their towers and saw that they were very tall and impressive. But they also saw that they were very wobbly and unstable. They had used too many blocks and not enough balance.

**Conclusion**  
Suddenly, a gust of wind blew through the window. It shook their towers and made them fall down. The blocks crashed and scattered all over the floor. Tom and Sam's towers were gone. They had nothing left to show for their work. They looked at each other and felt sad and angry. They had wasted their time and their blocks. They had hurt their brotherhood. They had learned a hard lesson. The moral of the story is: Don't be greedy and selfish. Share and cooperate with others. Be humble and respectful. Or you might lose everything you have.

**Conclusion**

**Introduction**  
Ben and Mia were friends. They liked to play together in the park. One day, they saw a big pot of soup on a table. It smelled good.

**Development**  
"Can we have some soup?" Ben asked Mia. "I don't know. Maybe we can borrow some," Mia said. They looked around. They did not see anyone near the table. They tiptoed to the pot. Ben reached for a spoon. Mia reached for a bowl. But they were clumsy. They knocked over the pot. The soup spilled everywhere. It was hot and sticky. They screamed. A lady came running. She was angry. She was the cook. She had made the soup for a picnic. "Who did this? Who ruined my soup?" she shouted. Ben and Mia were scared. They ran away. They hid behind a tree. 

**Conclusion**  
They felt sorry. They had made a big mess. They had not borrowed the soup. They had stolen it. They learned a lesson. They should not take things that are not theirs. They should ask first. They should be careful. They should say sorry.
